### SD7 Gap Analysis


**Sub-Domain:** SD7 Psychiatric Crisis Infrastructure
**Slug:** crisisInfra
**Date:** 2026-04-30
**Cycle:** RD2 first-pass; Gap Analysis SD7
**Authority:** RD2 §STEP 4; RD0 Standards 5.B/C/D/E
**Inputs:** track1-phase2-SD7; track2-phase2-SD7; substructure cross-cutting threads

---

#### §1 SD7 Gap Findings

##### G1-SD7-01: Right Care expansion as highest-impact infrastructure intervention

**Characterization.** Right Care (DBHIDS-PPD interagency mobile crisis + co-responder) operates with partial geographic + 24/7 coverage [F3/P1]. Coverage expansion is the highest-impact infrastructure intervention for crisis-system improvement per substructure-anticipated finding: it intervenes at the police-MH-crisis pathway entry point that produces upstream cumulative-disadvantage chain culmination. Expansion is operationally feasible (existing infrastructure; Home Rule-compatible); funding-sustainability + workforce-recruitment are constraints.

**Evidence trace.** T1P2 §6.2 (Right Care); T2P2 §4 Pathway B; substructure F3 + anticipated finding.
**Type:** [SD] structural derivation.
**Premises:** (1) Right Care current coverage documented as partial; (2) crisis-via-police pathway documented; (3) upstream cumulative-disadvantage chain documented; (4) Right Care intervention operates at the highest-leverage point in the chain.
**Confidence:** MEDIUM-HIGH for the leverage finding; specific FY26 coverage [F3/P1].
**Representation implication.** Federal-rep leverage at MIOTCRA + SAMHSA crisis-services grants; state-level lever at PA general fund + OMHSAS coordination; local at city budget + DBHIDS-PPD interagency expansion.

---

##### G1-SD7-02: Psychiatric boarding crisis as IMD-exclusion + state-hospital-saturation + private-bed-scarcity compound (SD2 + SD7 anchor)

**Characterization.** ED psychiatric boarding 24-72+ hour holds documented as systemic compound of (a) federal IMD exclusion (42 U.S.C. § 1396d(a)(B)) limiting Medicaid match for adult inpatient psychiatric facilities >16 beds; (b) PA state-hospital bed reduction over decades (current ~1,200-1,400 statewide; NSH = 375 forensic-only since 2019 per TC-05); (c) private psychiatric inpatient consolidation including Hahnemann July 2019 closure. Compound transmits to ED settings; not a procedural failure but a capacity failure.

**Evidence trace.** T1P2 §2.6 (IMD exclusion); T1P2 §6.4 (ED psychiatric capacity); SD2 G1-SD2-03 carry-through; T2P2 §2.1 (capacity baseline); T2P2 §4 Pathway A Step 4.
**Type:** [D] direct documentation of architecture + [SD] structural derivation of compound effect.
**Confidence:** HIGH for structural; MEDIUM-HIGH for current scale [F-verify FY26 boarding hour-distribution].
**Representation implication.** Federal-rep direct leverage at IMD exclusion modification; MIOTCRA + CCBHC expansion as community-services capacity inputs reducing inpatient demand; state-level lever at state-hospital sustainability. Cross-cutting + central to SD7 + SD2 finding integration.

---

##### G1-SD7-03: 988 dispatch rate gap (1-2% national; PA-3 to verify)

**Characterization.** SAMHSA Crisis Care Continuum framework (someone-to-call + someone-to-respond + safe-place-to-be) requires functional mobile-crisis dispatch to translate 988 calls to in-person crisis response. National dispatch rate documented at 1-2% [F7 → partially resolved per TC-08: PCL operates locally-based 988 in Philadelphia, ~6,000 calls/month; PCL-specific mobile dispatch rate remains a verification target]. National pattern means majority of 988 calls are resolved telephonically without in-person response; in-person response routes through 911/PPD as primary alternative — reproducing the police-pathway pattern that 988 was designed to alternative. The PCL co-location with 911 in Philadelphia (36% increase in 911→988 warm transfers per TC-08) partially structurally addresses this at PA-3 level.

**Evidence trace.** T1P2 §6.5; T2P2 §4 Pathway A Step 2; substructure F7.
**Type:** [SD] structural derivation from national pattern; PA-3 specific [F-verify].
**Premises:** (1) National 988 dispatch rate documented; (2) 988 architecture intends mobile-crisis-as-alternative-to-police; (3) low dispatch rate translates to police-as-default for in-person response; (4) the gap therefore reproduces upstream cumulative-disadvantage pattern.
**Confidence:** HIGH for national pattern; current PA-3 [F-verify].
**Representation implication.** Federal-rep leverage at SAMHSA 988 + crisis-services grants + Crisis Care Continuum implementation; state-level at PA OMHSAS 988 architecture + funding sustainability [F12]; local at DBHIDS-988 mobile-crisis dispatch capacity + Right Care integration.

---

##### G1-SD7-04: Crisis-via-police vs. crisis-via-self-direction racial pattern as cumulative racial-equity culmination

**Characterization.** Black PA-3 residents documented as more likely to enter crisis system through police pathway (police-initiated 302 + arrest-MH-pathway) than through self-direction or family-mediated pathway (988 self-call; CRC walk-in; ED self-presentation). The pattern is the entry-pathway expression of the cumulative-disadvantage chain that opens at SD3 (voluntary-utilization underutilization) + extends through SD2 (302 racial disparity) + SD6 (PPS overrepresentation + reentry-gap) + culminates at SD7 (crisis-via-police pathway dominance).

**Evidence trace.** T1P2 §7 (cumulative racial disadvantage); T2P2 §4 Pathways A + B comparison; G1-SD2-05; G1-SD3-03; G1-SD6-05.
**Type:** [SD] cross-domain pattern recognition.
**Confidence:** HIGH for the cumulative chain pattern; specific PA-3 quantification [F-verify].
**Representation implication.** Multi-level intervention required; resolution is the synthesis-level finding for D3. Federal-rep leverage spreads across multiple pathways: voluntary-system access (SD3); police-MH-crisis-response reform (Right Care + CIT; SD6 + SD7); commitment-system reform (SD2); crisis-system architecture (SD7). Single-lever intervention insufficient; coordinated multi-lever required.

---

Pediatric crisis pathway compound: ED boarding for pediatric MH; out-of-city placement family-separation effect; pediatric Medicaid-pathway-to-inpatient capacity constraint.

**Evidence trace.** T1P2 §6.4 (CHOP); T2P2 §4 Pathway C; SD4 cross-references.
**Type:** [SD] structural derivation.
**Confidence:** MEDIUM-HIGH; specific FY26 [F-verify].
**Representation implication.** Federal-rep leverage at SAMHSA pediatric crisis grants + CMS pediatric Medicaid + IMD-related pediatric considerations; state at PA pediatric BH capacity expansion; local at DBHIDS pediatric crisis-services coordination.

---

Comprehensive SAMHSA capacity erosion [F11 → resolved per TC-06] + CMS OBBBA implementation pressures [F13 → resolved per TC-03] + DOJ CRD posture shift threatens crisis-system architecture cumulatively. Multi-agency dependency means cumulative erosion compounds — single-agency restoration insufficient if peer agencies remain eroded.

**Evidence trace.** T1P2 §4 (multi-agency profile); SD3 + SD6 federal-agency analyses.
**Type:** [SD] structural derivation.
**Confidence:** HIGH for the architecture + vulnerability pattern; specific FY26 status [F11/F13/F-verify].
**Representation implication.** Federal-rep leverage at congressional appropriations + agency oversight across multiple agencies; restoration requires coordinated multi-agency approach. Cross-cutting to all D3 SDs.

## Domain Synthesis


**Date:** 2026-04-30
**Cycle:** RD2 first-pass; Step 5 Domain Synthesis
**Authority:** RD2 §STEP 5; RD0 Standards 5, 8, 12 (Step 5 inventory-only rule)
**Inputs:** all D3 SD1-SD7 Track 1 Phase 2, Track 2 Phase 2, Gap Analysis outputs

**Synthesis inventory note:** This synthesis operates under RD0 Standard 12.C / source-discipline Step 5 inventory-only rule. All specifics + named entities + dated events + cited authorities below appear in at least one underlying SD output (T1P2 / T2P2 / Gap) and are paired to source workspace entries. **No new specifics, sums, or aggregate counts are introduced.** Cross-SD pattern recognition + structural-derivation conclusions appear as such.

---

### §1 What This Domain Established

**One numbered finding per sub-domain (per RD2 synthesis structural template):**

**1. SD1 Mental Health Infrastructure & Governance (per SD1 cycle, completed prior session).** DBHIDS dual city/county authority + CBH single-MCO HealthChoices BH Home Rule innovation establishes the system-level institutional architecture within which SD2-SD7 operate. Per-capita MHBG inadequacy + Medicaid-capitation rate-sensitivity + dual-authority structure are the foundational architectural features of D3.

**2. SD2 Civil Commitment & Involuntary Treatment.** PA MHPA architecture is structurally permissive of involuntary commitment relative to peer-state procedural floors, with three constitutive features (no pre-detention judicial review for § 302; voluntary-to-involuntary § 305 conversion; § 304(f) outpatient commitment) addressable only by legislative reform. The 8-12K Philadelphia § 302 petition annual volume + § 303 hearing operational compression + psychiatric boarding crisis + police-initiated § 302 racial disparity (3-4× per national pattern applied) establish the entry-pathway link in the cumulative-racial-equity chain.

**3. SD3 Community Mental Health & SUD Treatment Services.** The gap between Olmstead-mandated community services + EPSDT entitlement + SUPPORT Act IMD parity and operational capacity (~2-3K ACT team waitlist; 4-8 week adult outpatient + 3-6 month child/adolescent specialty wait time; phantom-provider network adequacy; HPSA-MH cumulative-disadvantage geography correlation; X-waiver elimination implementation gap; OTP geographic + daily-dosing burden; SUD-MH treatment integration challenge) IS the central representation gap in D3. SD3 is the federal-rep-leverage-richest sub-domain in D3.

**4. SD4 Children's Behavioral Health.** Pediatric BH operates under a distinct entitlement architecture (EPSDT + IDEA + FFPSA + CCBHC + CASSP + Braidwood-preserved screening) that is structurally stronger than the adult community-services framework, but the multi-system youth integration challenge (Medicaid + schools + child welfare + juvenile justice) reproduces operational shortfall at the integration seams. ESSER III sustainability cliff (Sept 2024 expiration) + parallel BSCA $1B school-MH funding termination (April 29, 2025) [TC-02] + RTF placement geography (Philadelphia children placed outside city; racial-equity dimension) + BHRS-quality-shift unevenness are the four first-order representation gaps within otherwise robust formal architecture.

**5. SD5 Mental Health Parity.** Parity formally exists across multiple statutory layers (MHPAEA + ACA + CAA 2021 + Sept 2024 Final Rule + PA Mental Health Parity Act + Medicaid MCO rules) but operationally breaks down at three distinct architectural seams: NQTL enforcement gaps (network adequacy + prior-auth + medical-necessity criteria); ERISA preemption of self-funded employer plans (the anchor-institution-employee paradox); rate-driven access barrier in CBH that creates formal-compliance + operational-shortfall coexistence. Sept 2024 Final MHPAEA Rule was paused per Tri-Agency non-enforcement statement May 15, 2025 [TC-01]; the 2013 Rule + CAA 2021 statutory NQTL comparative-analysis obligations remain operative as the federal-rep enforcement infrastructure.

**6. SD6 Forensic Mental Health.** PPS as functionally Philadelphia's largest psychiatric facility (per verified PPS daily census FY25-26 ~3,500-3,700 × 12.6% SMI = ~440-470 daily SMI exceeds NSH 375 forensic-only beds [TC-04 + TC-05]) is the operational fingerprint of transinstitutionalization. Mental Health Court (~400-600/yr) + DBHIDS forensic unit + CIT (~25-30% PPD coverage) operate as diversion-and-reentry infrastructure that reaches a structurally small fraction of the eligible population. The reentry Medicaid suspension/reinstatement gap is the highest-leverage operational finding within the constitutional + statutory architecture (CAA 2023 § 5121 + CAA 2024 frameworks unchanged by OBBBA per TC-03).

**7. SD7 Psychiatric Crisis Infrastructure.** Crisis infrastructure is the operational nexus where SD2 + SD3 + SD4 + SD5 + SD6 capacity gaps converge. Right Care expansion is the highest-impact infrastructure intervention; psychiatric boarding crisis is the IMD-exclusion + state-hospital-saturation + private-bed-scarcity compound; 988 mobile-crisis dispatch rate (1-2% national; PA-3 [F-verify]) is the architecture-vs.-implementation gap; crisis-via-police pathway pattern is the cumulative racial-equity chain culmination. Crisis-system reform alone cannot resolve upstream-driven shortfall.

---

### §2 Aggregate Finding

D3's domain-level finding is **the legal-mandate / operational-shortfall gap as the central representation question of mental health in PA-3**. Olmstead, EPSDT, MHPAEA, county service obligations, MHPA procedural rights, 988 architecture — all create legal entitlements + protections. Funding inadequacy, workforce shortage, network adequacy gaps, ERISA preemption, Medicaid rate insufficiency, IMD exclusion, federal agency capacity erosion all create operational shortfall. The representation gap in mental health is principally in *delivery*, not in *legal design* — with isolated exceptions (§ 302 no-pre-judicial-review; ERISA self-funded gap; § 304(f) outpatient commitment limits) where legal-design-level reform is the lever.

Three structural patterns recur across sub-domains and integrate at this synthesis level:

**Pattern A: Transinstitutionalization as documented structural outcome.** The chain — deinstitutionalization (CMHC Act 1963 onward) → community infrastructure underbuilt → state-hospital bed reduction (PA from 30K+ public psychiatric beds to ~1,200-1,400) → SMI population displaced into emergency departments (SD2 + SD7 boarding), jails (SD6 PPS as functionally largest psychiatric facility), and homelessness — is the central macro-structural finding. Operational fingerprints: 8-12K § 302 petitions/yr (SD2); PPS ~440-470 SMI daily exceeds NSH 375 forensic-only beds (SD6) [verified per TC-04 + TC-05]; psychiatric boarding 24-72+ hour ED holds (SD2 + SD7); ED + CRC + crisis residential capacity strain (SD7).

**Pattern B: Cumulative racial-equity chain across sub-domains.** Black PA-3 residents experience: lower voluntary outpatient utilization (SD3) → higher police-initiated § 302 (SD2) → overrepresentation in PPS SMI population (SD6) → reentry-gap impact disproportionately Black + Hispanic (SD6) → crisis-via-police pathway pattern (SD7). Each step is independently documented + racially traceable; the chain compounds across multiple SDs. Provider-density geography correlates with redlined-mapped + Black-population-concentrated cumulative-disadvantage geography (SD3 G1-SD3-03; SD4 RTF placement; SD5 coverage architecture racial pattern). The chain is not a series of isolated disparities but a structural compound; resolution requires coordinated multi-lever intervention rather than single-lever reform.

**Pattern C: Multi-system coordination gap at the integration seams.** SD4 (CBH-SDP-DHS-juvenile probation seams) + SD6 (PPS-courts-DBHIDS-community-providers + reentry coordination) + cross-cutting through SD3 (DBHIDS-OMHSAS-DDAP architecture) document the same structural shape: formal entitlement architecture + operational coordination at agency-boundary seams produces shortfall. Multi-system coordination is therefore a domain-level structural finding, not a sub-domain artifact.

These three patterns are mutually reinforcing: transinstitutionalization (Pattern A) is racially patterned (Pattern B) and operates through multi-system coordination failures (Pattern C). The D3 representation question therefore integrates legal-mandate-vs.-operational-shortfall + transinstitutionalization + cumulative racial-equity + multi-system coordination as the four layers of analysis.

---

### §3 Confidence Calibration

Confidence ratings across D3 findings follow per-finding gap-analysis labels.

**HIGH confidence:** structural architectural findings (PA MHPA architecture; ERISA preemption framework; IMD exclusion; constitutional doctrine); pattern-recognition findings where multiple SDs independently document component steps (cumulative racial-equity chain; transinstitutionalization fingerprint; multi-system coordination shape).

**MEDIUM-HIGH confidence:** structural-derivation findings drawing on national patterns applied to PA-3 (police-initiated § 302 racial disparity 3-4×; out-of-network MH utilization 3-8×; ED placement racial disparity at SDP); pattern findings with current-PA-3 quantification flagged for verification.

**MEDIUM confidence + F-flagged:** specific FY26 quantifications (PPS census + MH rate; Right Care coverage; CRC visit volume; CIT coverage rate; MH Court capacity; 988 dispatch rate; ESSER replacement-funding picture; Sept 2024 Rule administrative status; Norristown bed capacity).

The first-pass cycle produces structural-quality + pattern-recognition findings at HIGH-MEDIUM-HIGH confidence; specific FY26 currency requires the verification cycle. The F-flag inventory is the operational interface between this first pass and the verification cycle.

### §6 Domain-Level Representation Question

**Element 1 (formal provision):** D3 formally provides comprehensive mental health entitlement + protection architecture: ADA Title II + Olmstead community-integration mandate; MHPAEA + ACA + CAA + Sept 2024 Final Rule parity; EPSDT pediatric entitlement + IDEA + FFPSA + CCBHC pediatric services; Medicaid behavioral health + IMD-exclusion-with-SUD-parity-erosion; PA MHPA civil commitment procedural protections; PA Mental Health Parity Act state floor; CBH HealthChoices BH MCO; DBHIDS dual city/county authority; 988 Lifeline + EMTALA emergency stabilization; CIT + Right Care + DBHIDS forensic unit diversion infrastructure; PAIMI advocacy authority. Multi-layer architecture.

**Element 2 (actual receipt):** Operational shortfall manifests in: 4-8 week adult + 3-6 month child psychiatry outpatient wait times; ~2-3K ACT team waitlist; phantom-provider network adequacy; psychiatric boarding 24-72+ hour ED holds; PPS as functionally largest psychiatric facility; reentry Medicaid suspension/reinstatement gap with first-2-4-weeks peak-crisis-risk window; 988 dispatch rate gap; Right Care zone + 24/7 coverage gap; CIT ~25-30% coverage; ERISA self-funded employer-plan parity gap; out-of-network MH utilization 3-8× medical/surgical; ESSER III school-based BH sustainability cliff; cumulative racial-equity chain culminating in crisis-via-police pathway dominance for Black PA-3 residents.

**Element 3 (account for the gap):** Multiple compounding causes: federal funding inadequacy (MHBG, SABG, SAMHSA discretionary capacity erosion [F11 → resolved per TC-06]); Medicaid rate inadequacy driving workforce shortage + network erosion; IMD exclusion structurally limiting inpatient capacity (architecture unchanged by OBBBA per TC-03; NACo H.R. 5462 / H.R. 6727 are active reform lever); ERISA preemption removing self-funded plans from state parity reach; deinstitutionalization-without-community-infrastructure producing transinstitutionalization; cumulative-disadvantage geography reproducing in service-quality + provider-density variation; federal agency capacity erosion across SAMHSA + CMS + DOJ CRD + ED OSEP threatening multi-agency architecture; OBBBA implementation phased pressure FY28-34 [TC-03]: state-directed payment caps + provider tax reduction; CCBHC/FQHC/RHC exemptions protect key D3 streams; IMD + reentry frameworks unchanged; provider workforce shortage upstream of network adequacy.

**Element 4 (meaning for representation analysis):** D3 represents **the legal-mandate / operational-shortfall gap as central representation question + transinstitutionalization as macro-structural finding + cumulative racial-equity chain as domain-level finding + multi-system coordination as cross-cutting structural pattern**. The four layers integrate at the domain level. Federal-rep leverage points (priority order): (1) Right Care expansion + 988 architecture coordinated investment (SD7 G1-SD7-01); (2) IMD exclusion modification (cross-cutting SD2 + SD3 + SD7; transinstitutionalization-relevant; **NACo-led legislative reform H.R. 5462 + H.R. 6727 are the active federal-rep lever per TC-03 — IMD architecture unchanged by OBBBA**); (3) Medicaid rate-setting + CCBHC PPS expansion (SD3 federal-rep-leverage-richest; **CCBHC/FQHC/RHC exemption from new OBBBA cost-sharing protects this stream per TC-03**); (4) MHPAEA enforcement infrastructure post-2024 Rule pause: continued enforcement of 2013 Rule + CAA 2021 NQTL comparative-analysis obligations; potential reactivation of 2024 Rule provisions if ERIC litigation resolves favorably (SD5 G1-SD5-03 [TC-01]); (5) MIOTCRA + Second Chance Act + § 1115 reentry waiver (SD6; **CAA 2023 § 5121 youth + CAA 2024 suspension-not-termination frameworks unchanged by OBBBA per TC-03**); (6) post-ESSER federal replacement-funding for school-based BH (SD4 G1-SD4-02; **parallel BSCA $1B school-MH funding termination April 29, 2025 per TC-02**; cross-domain to D11); (7) SAMHSA + CMS + ED OSEP + ACF + DOJ CRD agency capacity restoration (federal-rep architecture-level lever; **per TC-06 SAMHSA capacity erosion comprehensive: AHA reorganization March 27, 2025; >50% staff reduction; PAIMI administration team laid off**); (8) Olmstead enforcement + community-services investment integrated. State + local levers operative across all sub-domains; cumulative federal-rep-leverage richness is the SD3 + D3 finding (G1-XC-04). The D3 representation question is therefore answerable: **mental health representation in PA-3 has a robust legal-design floor that operates with documented operational shortfall traceable to multi-layer compounding causes, racially patterned across a cumulative-disadvantage chain, with high-leverage federal-rep intervention points concentrated at IMD exclusion + Medicaid rate-setting + crisis-system architecture + agency capacity restoration.**

- **SD5:** verify Sept 2024 Final MHPAEA Rule administrative status; DOL EBSA enforcement priorities FY26; PA Insurance Department enforcement; anchor-institution self-funded plan covered-life count + utilization; CBH parity attestation FY26
- **SD6:** verify PPS daily census + MH rate FY26; MH Court capacity + outcomes; CIT coverage rate; PA 1115 reentry waiver status; reentry Medicaid timing; PA-AOPC reporting
- **SD7:** verify Right Care zone coverage + 24/7 hours; 988 PA call + dispatch rate; CRC visit volume; crisis residential bed-day; ED psychiatric boarding hour-distribution

**Cross-cutting priorities:**

- Cumulative racial-equity chain quantification at each step with PA-3-specific data
- Federal agency capacity posture (SAMHSA + CMS + ED OSEP + ACF + DOJ CRD) FY26 cumulative
- OBBBA Medicaid implementation effects on BH capitation + CBH revenue
- DBHIDS FY26 budget allocation + ARPA replacement
- Sept 2024 MHPAEA Final Rule trajectory under Trump 2 admin

**Methodology refinements:**

- Continue file-first execution per RD-Efficiency-Override
- Maintain ≤4200-word per-SD depth cap with second-pass deepening through deepened aggregate pathways + integrated narrative rather than expanded structural sections
- Source-discipline str_replace workspace updates re-prioritized at second pass given the integrated-prose drafting pattern more amenable to per-claim pairing
- Anchor institution triple-role coverage deepening at SD5 (the architecturally-distinctive ERISA framing) as priority

## Summary of Material Changes

This section documents temporal changes between the first-pass draft date (2026-04-30) and the verification cycle (2026-04-30, completed same day via web verification of explicit verification flags F1–F15 and resolution of [VERIFY] tags). Format: four-field — what was framed in first-pass / what is verified state / when the change occurred / which G1 IDs are affected.

Material changes fall into three categories:
- **Federal-policy temporal changes** (TC-01 to TC-03, TC-06): policy/regulatory state changed between first-pass anchor framing and verification
- **Quantitative corrections** (TC-04, TC-07-partial): first-pass quantification was inaccurate or imprecise; verified figures supersede
- **Structural / naming corrections** (TC-05, TC-07-naming, TC-08, TC-09, TC-10): first-pass framing required correction or specification through verification

<a id="tc-01"></a>

### TC-01 — MHPAEA Sept 2024 Final Rule formally paused [P1]

- **What was:** Sept 2024 Final Rule effective Sept 9, 2024; status under Trump 2 admin uncertain (possible enforcement rollback, modification, or delay)
- **What is now:** Tri-Agencies (DOL, HHS, Treasury) issued joint non-enforcement statement May 15, 2025. Will not enforce 2024 Final Rule's NEW provisions until ERIC litigation resolves + 18 months. 2013 Rule + CAA 2021 statutory NQTL comparative-analysis obligation remain in force. Rule under "reconsideration." EO 14219 broader admin context.
- **When:** May 15, 2025 (DOL EBSA non-enforcement statement); preceded by *ERIC v. DOL/HHS/Treasury* filing Jan 17, 2025 in D.D.C. and abeyance grant May 12, 2025
- **Affects:** G1-SD5-01, G1-SD5-03, G1-SD5-05; F1, F14

- *Sources:* [S-04](#s-04), [S-23](#s-23), [S-27](#s-27), [S-39](#s-39), [S-44](#s-44)

<a id="tc-02"></a>

### TC-02 — Bipartisan Safer Communities Act $1B school-MH funding freeze [P1]

- **What was:** BSCA (P.L. 117-159, June 2022) school-based MH funding flowing as ESSER-replacement bridge
- **What is now:** Trump admin froze $1B of BSCA school-MH funding in 2025. KFF documents federal grant share for school MH dropped 53% (2021-22) → 33% (2024-25); 2,416 federal Department of Education employees lost jobs in 2025; 19 Community Schools 5-year grants terminated Dec 20, 2025
- **When:** Throughout 2025
- **Affects:** G1-SD4-02, G1-XC-04

- *Sources:* [S-10](#s-10), [S-50](#s-50), [S-86](#s-86)

<a id="tc-03"></a>

### TC-03 — OBBBA enacted as P.L. 119-21 [P1]

- **What was:** Federal Medicaid framework as established by ACA + 2018 SUPPORT Act + state-directed payment + provider-tax safe harbor at 6%; budget reconciliation pending
- **What is now:** One Big Beautiful Bill Act signed July 4, 2025. $1T Medicaid cuts over 10 years phased. Provider tax safe harbor 6%→3.5% (FY28-34, $191B savings). State-directed payment caps (~$149B). Work requirements + 6-month redeterminations effective Dec 30, 2026 (HHS extension authority to 2028). CBO: 11.8M lose coverage by 2034. **CCBHCs/FQHCs/RHCs EXEMPTED** from new $1-$35 cost-sharing. **Section 71401 Rural Health Transformation Program** $10B/yr 2026-2030. House gender-affirming-care funding ban STRUCK by Senate Parliamentarian. **IMD exclusion (42 U.S.C. § 1396d(a)(B)) UNCHANGED**. **Reentry continuity preserved** (CAA 2023 § 5121 youth + CAA 2024 suspension-not-termination unchanged).
- **When:** July 4, 2025 (signed); implementation phased through 2034
- **Affects:** G1-SD1-02, G1-SD3-01, G1-SD6-02, G1-XC-04; F4, F13

- *Sources:* [S-13](#s-13), [S-87](#s-87), [S-88](#s-88), [S-89](#s-89), [S-91](#s-91), [S-92](#s-92)

<a id="tc-04"></a>

### TC-04 — PPS daily census quantification correction [P1, central D3 finding]

- **What was:** PPS daily census ~4,500-5,500; SMI subset ~10-15% = ~450-825 daily SMI
- **What is now:** Verified daily census FY25-26: April 2025 = 3,575; **May 2025 = 3,461 (lowest in 33 years since April 17, 1992)**; November 2025 = 3,674. SMI: **12.6% of total jail population** (FJD 2022; Urban Institute SJC). 12.6% × ~3,500-3,700 = **~440-470 daily SMI** — narrows first-pass 450-825 range to LOW END. PDP transitioned to new jail management system Fall 2024 (data gap Oct 2024-Feb 2025); March 2025 onward 12 confinement categories with race+ethnicity reported separately.
- **When:** Trend ongoing 2024-2026; "lowest in 33 years" milestone May 15, 2025
- **Affects:** G1-SD6-01 (central D3 finding — claim now requires narrower margin framing); G1-SD6-02 (releases volume needs FY26 update); G1-SD6-05 (race/ethnicity now reported separately under new PDP system); F6
- **Implication:** "PPS as functionally largest psychiatric facility" claim HOLDS but at narrower margin than first-pass implied (PPS SMI ~440-470 vs. NSH 375 forensic-only beds = ~65-95 margin) — see also TC-05

- *Sources:* [S-78](#s-78), [S-79](#s-79), [S-82](#s-82), [S-84](#s-84), [S-85](#s-85), [S-101](#s-101)

<a id="tc-05"></a>

### TC-05 — Norristown State Hospital structurally narrowed; civil-only since 2019 [P2]

- **What was:** NSH ~300 beds; civil §304 long-term commitments routed to NSH (geographic burden for Philadelphia families)
- **What is now:** NSH = **375 beds total, 100% forensic** (255 Regional Forensic Psychiatric Center + 120 Forensic Stepdown). **Civil section closed permanently January 2019** (Feb 2018 announcement). Civil §304 commitments from Philadelphia now route to **Danville State Hospital (Montour County)** — geographic burden EXACERBATED at different facility. NSH is the only remaining state psychiatric facility in southeastern PA. Forward-looking: Stantec + architecture+ designing new Southeast Psychiatric Treatment Center; initial 270 single-occupant beds expanding to 420 total; groundbreaking 2026.
- **When:** January 2019 (civil section closure); predates first-pass cycle but was inaccurately characterized in first-pass
- **Affects:** G1-SD2-06 (long-term commitment family-contact externality now relates to Danville, not NSH); G1-SD6-01 (PPS-larger-than-NSH claim — holds at narrower margin; future 420-bed expansion will narrow further); F5

- *Sources:* [S-43](#s-43), [S-69](#s-69), [S-102](#s-102)

<a id="tc-06"></a>

### TC-06 — SAMHSA agency capacity comprehensive erosion [P1, Pattern 1]

- **What was:** SAMHSA capacity erosion threading anticipated as Pattern 1 risk (G1-SD1-05, G1-XC-02)
- **What is now:** HHS placed SAMHSA under **Administration for a Healthy America (AHA)** under RFK Jr. SAMHSA had ~900 staff start-2025; major RIFs throughout 2025. Children's Mental Health Initiative team on admin leave; Minority Fellowship Program admin branch laid off; **PAIMI administration team laid off** (despite Congress maintaining funding). FY26 budget proposal: ~$6.5-7B (down from ~$7.5-8B). Proposed block grant consolidation: SUPTRS + CMHS + SOR → "Behavioral Health Innovation Block Grant" $4.126B (down $465M). NIH consolidation: NIAAA+NIDA+NIMH → "National Institute of Behavioral Health" at $2.678B (down $1.86B). **Jan 14-15, 2026 episode**: HHS terminated $2B in ~2,000 SAMHSA grants overnight via "non-alignment" letters; reversed 24 hours later after White House intervention. 23-state lawsuit over $11B+ public health funding rescissions. HHS contingency plan: 21% staff (123) retained as excepted in shutdown.
- **When:** Throughout 2025; major operational events Jan 14-15, 2026
- **Affects:** G1-SD1-05 (Pattern 1 thread); G1-XC-02 (MAHA Pattern 1); G1-XC-04 (federal-rep-leverage richness); F11

- *Sources:* [S-05](#s-05), [S-09](#s-09), [S-45](#s-45), [S-46](#s-46), [S-47](#s-47), [S-90](#s-90), [S-94](#s-94), [S-95](#s-95), [S-103](#s-103), [S-104](#s-104)

<a id="tc-07"></a>

### TC-07 — DBHIDS leadership, FY26 budget, and program-naming clarification [P1]

- **What was:** "Right Care (DBHIDS-PPD interagency mobile crisis + co-responder)" operates with partial geographic + 24/7 coverage; DBHIDS budget framework not specified; DBHIDS commissioner not specified
- **What is now:** Program terminology corrected — **Community Mobile Crisis Response Teams (CMCRT)** (civilian-only, 4 nonprofit providers Elwyn / PATH / Consortium / JFK, 29 teams 24/7 across Philadelphia) and **Crisis Intervention Response Team (CIRT)** (police+civilian co-responder, 911-routed) are **SEPARATE** programs. CMCRT response time 50 min avg (down from 60+; goal 30). ~14,000 dispatches Jan 2023 - Jan 2025. 70%+ user satisfaction. **17% decrease in involuntary commitment referrals.** Pilot started 2021 after Walter Wallace Jr. death (Oct 2020). DBHIDS Interim Commissioner since April 2024: **Marquita Williams**. CBH CEO: **Donna E.M. Bailey, MSEd, MBA**. DBHIDS FY26 budget request: ~$21.5M BH programming; $1.8M MH Court Evaluations new; $500K outreach team new. Mayor Parker administration attempting to sever $3.8M Consortium contract over tax-exempt default; Consortium appealing; still operating as of testimony.
- **When:** April 2024 (Williams interim appointment); 2024-25 (Parker-Consortium contract dispute); FY26 budget request submitted May 2025; CMCRT/CIRT distinction operational since 2021 Crisis 2.0 reorganization (predates first-pass cycle but mischaracterized)
- **Affects:** G1-SD7-01 (terminology + scaling correction); F3, F15
- **Note:** "Right Care" terminology not verified in current DBHIDS sources — may be project shorthand or historical name (see unverified-items UV-03)

- *Sources:* [S-73](#s-73), [S-74](#s-74), [S-75](#s-75), [S-76](#s-76), [S-77](#s-77), [S-80](#s-80), [S-99](#s-99), [S-100](#s-100)

<a id="tc-08"></a>

### TC-08 — Philadelphia Crisis Line (PCL) operational data [P2]

- **What was:** 988 dispatch rate gap framed as G1-SD7-03 (dispatch-rate-to-988 partial gap, no operational data)
- **What is now:** PCL is the **only locally-based, in-house 988 response team in the country**. 6,000+ calls/month. **988 counselors co-located in 911 Radio Room → 36% increase in warm transfers from 911 to 988**. **4,288 calls transferred 911 → PCL Jan 2023 - Feb 2025**. PCL inception January 2023.
- **When:** PCL inception January 2023; ongoing operational scaling through FY26 budget request
- **Affects:** G1-SD7-03 (988 dispatch gap — PCL coverage now documented as national-leading model); F7, F12 (partial resolution at PA-3 level)

- *Sources:* [S-48](#s-48), [S-74](#s-74)

<a id="tc-09"></a>

### TC-09 — DRP active with major recent settlements [P2]

- **What was:** DRP active in P&A role; specific recent activity not specified in first-pass
- **What is now:** DRP filed 2 settlements directly relevant to D3 in 2025:
  (a) **October 1, 2025**: M.D. Pa. (Hon. Julia K. Munley) approved DRP-DHS settlement on 2017 class action re children with MH disabilities adjudicated dependent. Requires DHS to improve timely MH screenings, multi-system teaming, prevent under-10 RTF placement, with independent Consultant oversight.
  (b) **March 24, 2025**: Settlement reached on DRP 2019 suit alleging civil rights violations at PA Youth Development Centers (~70% of YDC youth have disabilities; abusive restraints + MH care deprivation alleged).
  DRP Litigation Counsel: Rhonda Brownstein. Active intake 6,800+ callers annually. Note: SAMHSA's federal PAIMI administering branch was laid off in 2025 (TC-06) — funding flow continues but federal admin support eroded.
- **When:** March 24, 2025 (YDC settlement); October 1, 2025 (dependent children settlement)
- **Affects:** G1-SD4-01, G1-SD4-04, G1-SD6-04; F10

- *Sources:* [S-05](#s-05), [S-40](#s-40), [S-41](#s-41), [S-96](#s-96), [S-97](#s-97), [S-98](#s-98)

<a id="tc-10"></a>

### TC-10 — PA HealthChoices BH capitation increase proposed; Act 98 telehealth permanence [P2]

- **What was:** PA HealthChoices BH framework stable; rate trajectory unspecified; outpatient telehealth restrictions context
- **What is now:** Governor Shapiro 2026-27 budget proposes **15% increase to $4.4 billion** in Medicaid capitation funding for behavioral and physical HealthChoices programs combined. CBH continues as Philadelphia's exclusive BH-MCO under HealthChoices (CEO Donna E.M. Bailey; 420,000 Medicaid recipients). **PA Act 98 of 2022** permanently removed audio-only telehealth restrictions in outpatient psych + D&A clinic settings.
- **When:** Shapiro 2026-27 budget proposal (early 2026, contingent on enactment); Act 98 enacted 2022 (predates first-pass cycle but not surfaced)
- **Affects:** G1-SD1-08, G1-SD3-02 (telehealth permanence partial mitigation), G1-SD5-06; F8

### Verification cycle notes

**Searches conducted (Step 3, 2026-04-30):** 10 web searches resolving 9 of 9 P1 verification flags (F1, F2, F3, F6, F8, F11, F13, F14, F15) and 5 of 7 P2 flags fully (F4, F5, F7, F10, F12 — F7 + F12 via PCL data; F4 via OBBBA tracking). One P2 flag (F9 PA Act 30 implementation status) remains unresolved — see unverified-items.

**P1 vs. P2 prioritization rationale:** P1 flags (high-impact representational claims) were resolved comprehensively; P2 flags (lower-impact framing or specifics) were addressed within budget; one P2 deferred. SDP-specific FY26 BH workforce data also remains incompletely verified — architectural framework verified (ESSER cliff + BSCA freeze) but SDP-specific FY26 workforce numbers were not surfaced.

**Pattern stability:** First-pass macro-architectural patterns — Pattern A transinstitutionalization (synthesis §3); Pattern B cumulative racial-equity chain (synthesis §3); Pattern C multi-system coordination gap (synthesis §3); plus the cross-cutting Pattern 1 federal capacity erosion thread (G1-SD1-05; verified comprehensive per TC-06) — were strongly confirmed by verification. Note: the §Verification cycle notes label scheme has been aligned to the synthesis Pattern A/B/C canonical scheme; the prior "Pattern 1/2/3" enumeration is retained only for the federal-capacity-erosion thread (Pattern 1) consistent with D2-V usage. The central D3 macro-finding ("PPS as functionally largest psychiatric facility in southeastern PA") was confirmed but at narrower quantitative margin than first-pass implied (PPS ~440-470 SMI daily vs. NSH 375 forensic-only beds; ~65-95 margin per TC-04 + TC-05). The federal-rep-leverage architecture identified in first-pass G1-XC-04 was sharpened by verification rather than disturbed.

**Unverified items carried forward:** See `unverified-items_D3_2026-04-30.md` for items not resolved within search budget. Items carried forward as documented limitations rather than blocking issues.

**Remediation pass (2026-04-30):** Body content updated inline per `D3_verified_remediation_spec_2026-04-30.md` to comply with M2 §7's "corrections applied inline" specification, addressing the inline-integration failure identified in `D3_verified_audit_report_FINAL_2026-04-30.md`. Approximately 110+ body locations modified across 10 TCs, including 14 principal-anchor italicized prior-pass notes (one per TC, with secondary anchors for TC-01, TC-04, TC-05, TC-07), ~80 secondary value swaps, and ~25 F-flag status updates. Per remediation spec §0.3, "Right Care" terminology retained as project-internal shorthand at non-anchor body references; principal anchors at SD1 §6.3 and SD7 §6.2 establish the CMCRT/CIRT verified terminology. PA MHPA section-number labeling reconciliation (audit §5.1 / spec §11.1) deferred — pre-existing input artifact, not remediation-introduced. Material Changes Summary (below) preserved unchanged as canonical record of what changed and why.

**Category decisions worth flagging**

- §III entries mixed three sub-types: CFR entries (Cat 1), Federal Register final rule (Cat 1), one EO (Cat 2), and one federal agency statement (Cat 4). These were disaggregated: CFR/Fed. Reg. entries stay in Cat 1 (S-20 to S-26); DOL EBSA Statement moved to Cat 4 (S-44); EO 14219 moved to Cat 2 (S-27).
- §I mixed court decisions and constitutional provisions. Constitutional provisions disaggregated to Cat 1 (U.S. Const. amend. XIV → S-01) and Cat 5 (PA Const. Art. I § 1 → S-52; PA Const. Art. I § 9 → S-53). Court decisions remain in Cat 3 (S-28–S-38).
- §X entries NPR and EMS1/CQ Roll Call were moved from §X (Reports) to Cat 7 (Press/News), as they are news articles, not research/policy reports. Assigned S-103 and S-104, falling within the Cat 7 S-99–S-104 range.
- §XI DRP entries (DRP corporate site + two settlement press releases) assigned to Cat 6 (Research Reports/Policy Analysis) rather than Cat 8 (Other Primary Sources) since DRP functions as an evidence source for claims about legal outcomes, not as a primary institutional actor in the domain.
- §IX Anchor Institutions / Provider Network entries assigned to Cat 8 (Other Primary Sources). These are primarily institutional identifications used to substantiate CMCRT provider roster and hospital-network claims; they do not have publishable research outputs.
- PDP Commissioner Michael R. Resnick (§VII row) consolidated into S-79 (PDP Press Release, May 20, 2025) since the Commissioner is identified only within that press release and is not a separate source. This is the one consolidation reducing 117 original rows to 116 S-NN entries.

**URL handling**
